# MMM research directions

## Prospective Research Baseline

2026-10-05: the prospective primary accuracy reference is now **Adaptive**,
selected from the complete consumed native cohort within the unchanged 400-ms
whole-synthetic-core ceiling. **Full** remains the speed control. The executable
[registry](research/baselines.json) and [research index](docs/research/README.md)
record the scope and fresh 240-arm replication. Historical frozen protocols,
old controls, negative results, and all seven whole-goal requirements remain
unchanged. This is not a deployed daemon default or untouched confirmation.
V83 remains the current numerically repaired regime candidate, without a
comparable broad-cohort quality result or accuracy promotion over Adaptive.
The earlier BLOCKED observation is historical. The goal controller was directly
rechecked ACTIVE on 2026-10-05 after the user-provided continuation; the latest
entry below records new work, not a change to any original success criterion.

## Current Continuation

Publication note: the public capture-load checkpoint below is now authorized for
publication on the curated research branch. Raw profiler captures remain local;
their omission is documented in `research/public-capture-load-v1/PUBLICATION.json`.
The original continuation notes describe the state at collection time. No
unfinished shard-rescue work or production promotion is included in this push.

2026-10-05 latest: [full-frontier public capture load](research/public-capture-load-v1/RESULTS.md)
and [two-worker rejection](research/public-capture-load-v1/WORKER_CAP_RESULTS.md).
Previous goal turn PROGRESS (audited local checkpoint published). This turn
PROGRESS: unchanged loaded comparison, eight public capture/frontier runs,
post-screen CPU/block/mutex diagnosis and eight frozen worker-cap rescue runs.
All seven WHOLE goals remain OPEN/ACTIVE; weekly usage rechecked at 46%.

The old one-event loaded fixture passes absolute finite gates but fails one
paired quiet non-regression cell (ratio 1.1633). In the new 1,000-replica public
DESIGN workload, all functional availability/publication/ledger checks pass,
but all ordinary runs miss recall p99 <100 ms: 114.56-127.88 ms at frontier 50,
156.88-176.37 ms at frontier 200. Mask reuse also fails one paired frontier-200
non-regression cell (ratio 1.1188). It remains a component optimization only.

The measured profile identifies capture-released reader locks and HNSW index
construction as the main new lead, not mask scans. A two-worker cap is rejected:
full recall p99 is 2.16-2.44x the explicit ten-worker control, with the same
capture bytes and almost identical overlap. No scored candidate, durable write
or guard was dropped to manufacture a pass. Every failed cell is preserved.
All 64 worker labels per run publish live before Close; 128 durable rows and
same-epoch replay agree. These are synthetic mechanics labels, not real agent
utility or cross-epoch learning transfer. The count of 288 public inputs is a
DESIGN capture-template count, not independent facts; the frozen runner's
misnamed field is explicitly corrected in the audit, not used for inference.

Next structural investigation: transaction-compatible incremental or smaller
partition index publication, preserving full merged retrieval, chronology,
atomic event/runtime publication and reopen/invalidation checks. A small-corpus
Flat-index control is not a replacement for large-corpus ANN serving. Visible
mutations, transfer/recovery, sustained backlog, all other original goals and
the .698246 TV defect remain unresolved. Production, private data, whitepaper
and published branches are untouched by this continuation; retain a local
scoped checkpoint, not an automatic publication or deployed setting change.

A separately recorded post-hoc exact Bernoulli recursion finds .003995
aligned expected proposal harm under the known law; this does not override

outcome trajectories and calibrated-prior controls precede loaded integration.

2026-10-02 [native batch journal v15](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-batch-journal-v15-results.md)

2026-10-02 [journal gate phase timing v14](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-journal-phases-v14-results.md)
locates the single-owner Goal 6 capacity cost below v13's 6.9 ms

agree on duplicate and crash-interruption controls. Two loaded
normal trials keep 128/128 durable writes and Recalls but **FAIL**
<100 ms offer p99 at 510/525 ms. Next test a genuinely different,

2026-10-02 [full-Recall phase timing v13](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-phases-v13-results.md)
identifies the current Goal 6 throughput constraint: under the unchanged
4 ms offered workload, journal owner wait p50 is 12.7 ms and durable
append p50 is 6.9 ms per Recall, versus about 27 ms occupied-call
p50. All 128/128 Recalls and writes remain correct in two normal
and two race-correctness trials, but offer p99 is 545/538 ms and
**FAILS** <100 ms. A single 6.9 ms serial journal lane cannot sustain
250 offers/s. Next separate the append gate's own costs and test a
different durable publication architecture; the older 8 ms-dwell
group journal already failed. All seven whole goals OPEN; production
untouched.

2026-10-02 [full-Recall profile v11](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-profile-v11-results.md)
and [empty-residual ablation v12](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-residual-ablation-v12-results.md)
rule out one easy Goal 6 capacity rescue. V11 found up to 300
residual point reads per 150-nominee Recall in the zero-residual
fixture, but v12 removing that work reduced median occupied-call
time only about 1 ms and offer p99 to 502/503 ms from a fresh v10
control's 521/529 ms. Both v12 trials still **FAIL** the <100 ms
response gate, despite 128/128 correct durable Recalls and zero
stale rejections. Next collect per-Recall journal, Search, admission
and service spans; aggregate blocking profiles do not prove the
dominant critical path. All seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [read-to-journal admission v10](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-admission-v10-results.md)
**PARTIALLY rescues** Goal 6 full-Recall completion: two normal
4 ms offered-load trials now have 128/128 durable writes and Recalls,
zero stale retries, exact pinned top-150/journal consistency, writer
age p99 85-88 ms and call p99 62-63 ms. It **FAILS** the unchanged
<100 ms offered-response gate: p99 516/557 ms, with four workers
capacity-limited by roughly 26-28 ms median occupied calls. The
predeclared race correctness mode passes both trials. Next measure
stage costs under the same load, then test a different architecture;
do not relabel this as serving validation. All seven whole goals OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-02 [published-LSN full Recall load v9](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-load-v9-results.md)
**FAILS** its frozen Goal 6 service screen in two normal trials:
128/128 writes but only 118/128 and 115/128 successful Recalls,
91/96 stale-snapshot rejections, and Recall offer-to-done p99
535/516 ms despite backend read p99 under 6 ms in v7. Successful
packets matched the versioned top-150 oracle and durable journals.
Predeclared race-correctness mode also fails completion (124/128
Recalls in both trials), with no reported data race or oracle leak.
Prior group-journal batching did not rescue capacity. A bounded
read-to-journal admission candidate is next, with writer freshness
and durable order preserved. All seven whole goals OPEN; production
untouched.

2026-10-02 [published-LSN Service Recall v8](docs/experiments/mmm-published-recall-v8-results.md)
PASSES a frozen deterministic, event-only Goal 6 integration: one
as-of-visible interleaved write is rejected on the old per-call pin,
then retried with matching packet/durable-journal snapshots and all
eligible nominees; a future-only write is safely ignored at that
as-of without retry or gate poisoning. Service policy binding must occur
before READY publication; the first invalid setup proved the gate catches
a later raw policy LSN move. Focused race and regression checks pass.
This does not establish loaded multiworker Recall, changing graph or
posterior reads, label-to-forecast freshness or agent outcomes. All seven
whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [dense 256D loaded published-LSN read v7](docs/experiments/mmm-published-dense-load-v7-results.md)
has a **split outcome** under its frozen Goal 6 backend screen. Two
normal executions pass all four k50/k200 offered-load trials, with
1,280/1,280 oracle-correct reads and durable acknowledgements combined,
read-call p99 at most 5.195 ms, write-age p99 at most 31.160 ms,
and max published-view age 28.410 ms. The race-instrumented run reports
no data race or integrity mismatch but **fails** the frozen write-age
gate (p99 1.032-1.295 s) under the same 4 ms schedule. No retuning.
Full Recall, Bayesian journal co-publication, learned-state freshness,
large corpus and agent outcomes remain untested; all seven whole goals
OPEN, production untouched.

2026-10-02 [dense 256D published-LSN top-k transfer v6](docs/experiments/mmm-published-dense-v6-results.md)
PASSES a frozen test-only Goal 6 retrieval component on 200 eligible/17
future dense 256D rows: at k=10, 50 and 200, journal-certified SQL and
ordinary Search have identical sets and cosine scores, with no future
leak. Quiet SQL p99 was 0.416/0.634/1.144 ms versus ordinary Search
0.726/0.884/2.060 ms in 32-call samples. No concurrent publication,
full Recall+journal, loaded service, large-corpus, or old-vector migration
claim follows. All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [certified-LSN top-k transfer v5](docs/experiments/mmm-published-topk-v5-results.md)
PASSES a frozen, test-only Goal 6 retrieval component: at k=10, 50 and
200, published-LSN SQL and ordinary Search have identical candidate sets
and cosine scores over 200 eligible/17 future rows, with no future leak.
Quiet four-dimensional SQL p99 was 0.346/0.502/1.099 ms versus ordinary
Search 0.772/0.878/1.897 ms; these are 32-call max samples and omit
publication, journal, full Recall and offered load. Full Goal 6 and all
seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [certified-LSN normalized write/query boundary v4](docs/experiments/mmm-published-normalized-boundary-v4-results.md)
PASSES a frozen, test-only Goal 6 component on nonunit caller vectors:
normalize before write and query, reject invalid vectors and mixed batches
atomically, then decode bodies and convert SQL cosine distances at the same
certified LSN. Five eligible similarities match ordinary Search before and
after reopen, with old/new LSN membership and future exclusion checked.
Existing nonunit-row migration, production enforcement, large top-k parity,
full Recall/journal coherence and loaded latency are not proven. Goal 6 and
all seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [certified-LSN payload and score probes](docs/experiments/mmm-published-payload-v1-results.md)
PASSES declared-schema EventFrame body projection at old/new certified
LSNs and after reopen, while the availability-only schema cannot bind
`event_json`. Direct SQL `<->` score reuse
[FAILS](docs/experiments/mmm-published-score-v1-results.md); SQL cosine
`<=>` plus `1-distance` also
[FAILS](docs/experiments/mmm-published-cosine-v2-results.md) for nonunit
stored vectors. A strictly [unit-normalized candidate](docs/experiments/mmm-published-unit-cosine-v3-results.md)
PASSES five finite score contrasts but has no enforced write/query contract
or migration. Full coherent Service Recall, learner freshness, and loaded
Goal 6 remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [concurrent pre-search snapshot screen](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-presearch-concurrent-v1-results.md)
**FAILS** its frozen Goal 6 completion and latency gates in two fresh
rotated 4 ms/4-worker service runs. The pin has zero observed silent
omissions but only 125/128 and 124/128 successful Recalls, with offer-to-
response p99 434.554/437.742 ms. The unchanged order succeeds 128/128
but silently omits its own visible event in 44/128 and 45/128 packets.
An adjacent [cross-tenant probe](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-cross-tenant-retry-v1-results.md)
confirms a visible B write forces A to retry even when A's candidates are
unchanged. Race run found no data race but failed the same gate. No
published-LSN/full-service integration or production change; Goal 6 and
all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [pre-search snapshot repeated-write service screen](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-presearch-load-v1-results.md)
PASSES a serial, test-only Goal 6 consistency component in two 3-arm runs:
96/96 unchanged-order Recalls per run silently omitted an as-of-visible
event while claiming its snapshot; 96/96 pre-search-pin Recalls per run
retried and included both events with matching durable journals. Pin/write
raw p99 was 16.911/15.769 ms; the incorrect control was 19.240/14.105 ms,
so the timing order flipped and no latency win follows. No concurrent
serving, published-LSN/full-Recall integration, or production change;
Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [full Recall Search/Snapshot interleaving](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-search-snapshot-v1-results.md)
**FAILS** on both memory and LibraVDB: a visible write between Search and
Snapshot yields one old candidate with packet and durable journal claiming
the newer runtime version. The separate test-only
[pre-search pin](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-presearch-snapshot-v1-results.md)
PASSES the same finite interleaving by making the existing journal guard
reject and retry; focused race, ordinary service tests and vet pass. This
does not establish a coherent external/published-LSN read view or loaded
Goal 6 performance. Production untouched; all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [Recall journal coexistence v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-journal-coexistence-v1-results.md)
**FAILS**: the real frontier journal moves the global DB LSN21->24 without
EventFrame snapshot motion, invalidating the event-only READY marker and
blocking a later authorized append. A separate test-only
[journal-through-gate transition](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-journal-gate-v1-results.md)
PASSES finite new/duplicate/interruption/reopen/bypass controls, race checks
and isolated journal cost (p50 5.020 ms, p99 6.535 ms). This needs exclusive
gate-only writers and is not wired into full Recall; direct-path failure
remains. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [last-published-LSN read rescue v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-published-view-v1-results.md)
PASSES two fresh three-arm, 4 ms offered-rate private backend screens:
batch/published read p99 3.226/3.423 ms versus single/original
11.273/11.684 ms, batched writer age p99 32.841/32.843 ms, maximum
view age 26.099/26.969 ms, zero integrity violations. Race correctness
passes. The [original batch gate](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-batch-openloop-v1-results.md)
FAILED its read-tail ratio twice (1.280/1.270), and the
[phase profile](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-batch-read-phases-v1-results.md)
localizes most of the tail to capture-lock wait. This rescue needs one
gate-only writer and has not passed full Recall, learner, restart-scale or
external-writer tests. Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven whole
goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [batch incremental publication v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-batch-publication-v1-results.md)
PASSES finite test-only batch journal, exact as-of, duplicate, interruption,
reopen and bypass controls. A deterministic same-Store interleaving first
**FAILED** for both batch and single gates; pairing LSN with runtime snapshot
and checking exact new-event motion fixes both regressions. At 1,027 rows,
eight 16-event groups cost 4.255 s as journaled singles versus 0.262 s as
journaled batches (0.062 ratio), with quiet capture p99 13.708 us. This is
isolated DB work, not offered-rate freshness, loaded Recall or per-event
immediate acknowledgement. Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven
whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [incremental publication append scale v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-append-scale-v1-results.md)
PASSES the frozen isolated correctness diagnostic but is a negative Goal 6
scaling result for the current one-event writer. At 35/259/1,027 starting
rows, raw write p50 was 6.852/11.977/32.982 ms and journaled write p50
was 7.100/12.314/33.201 ms. The journal is a small part of the measured
cost; raw Store append dominates as rows grow. Quiet capture p99 remains
under 18 us. No loaded Recall, sustained offered-rate or large-corpus claim
follows; a different batched/admission architecture needs its own as-of and
acknowledgement proof. Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven whole
goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [incremental publication startup scale v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-startup-scale-v1-results.md)
PASSES a frozen private diagnostic at 35, 259 and 1,027 rows. Across five
rotated pairs per size, verified Open p50 rises 2.541->9.602->35.968 ms;
candidate/control p50 ratios are 1.111/1.182/1.209. The journal verifier
adds cost but does not dominate ordinary Open in these finite fixtures.
Startup still verifies O(N) rows, and no large-corpus restart or loaded
service claim follows. Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven whole
goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [incremental sort-key publication v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-incremental-publication-v1-results.md)
PASSES a test-only **single-owner** Goal 6 component. Receipt-bound new rows
advance a SQLite journal and READY marker atomically without per-append
corpus scan; injected DB-only and SQLite-before-memory interruptions fail
closed, and reopen verifies the journal. Subsecond as-of, duplicate,
tamper, legacy-write and bypassed-sortable-write controls pass normally
and under race. Final isolated 100-append p99 was 8.742 ms; quiet capture
p99 134.333 us (versus 10.708 us in an earlier run). Startup validation
is O(N), DB-only recovery and enforced
exclusive ownership are absent, and no loaded Recall claim follows.
Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [private serial writer handoff v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-writer-serial-v1-results.md)
PASSES the adjacent Goal 6 control: fully closing the first Store before a
second opens preserves a new unkeyed event at LSN21->25 and makes the old
READY marker deny on reopen; full scan refuses republish. Normal and race
runs pass. Together with the dual-open failure below, this supports a
single long-lived owner requirement, not restart-per-event or concurrent
Store access. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-02 [private writer ownership v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-writer-ownership-v1-results.md)
**FAILS** the frozen dual-open Goal 6 probe. A second Store wrote the same
private LibraVDB path despite an advisory owner lock, reporting LSN25 while
the first Store still captured READY at LSN21. After second-then-first close,
reopen returned LSN21 with the acknowledged row absent. The stale capture
is not a proven future-data leak; the acknowledged-write loss and absent
database-enforced fence rule out simultaneous independent Opens as a safe
renewal scheme here. Normal and race runs reproduced it. Next test serial
handoff and require exclusive single-owner wiring before marker renewal.
Production untouched; Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-02 [sortable receipt v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sortable-receipt-v1-results.md)
passes a research-only exact-commit-LSN contract and a frozen three-pair
isolated writer cost screen. All p50 ratios are <=1.006 and p99 ratios
<=1.057 versus the existing sortable writer; pooled p50/p99 are
6.995/9.003 ms. Failing controls found both the missing-field enum-zero
trap and numeric-column coercion; writer and readiness now check the
durable catalog type and the exact-LSN SQL bind. Migration and publication
reopen tests pass again. Receipt alone does not solve cross-process writer
fencing, marker renewal, crash recovery or loaded service latency. Goal 6
and all seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [private sort-key publication v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-key-publication-v1-results.md)
passes a finite close/reopen PENDING-to-READY gate for a migrated
three-row collection. Wrong digest, incomplete backfill, absent sidecar,
post-READY legacy write and newer-LSN/old-digest marker all deny;
the exact-LSN read excludes a future subsecond row. A failing regression
found an in-memory LSN-alias bug, now corrected in the test-only gate.
Quiet gate p99 was 31.75 us over 1,000 checks; race and package checks pass.
No power-loss, multiprocess writer fence, incremental authorized renewal or
loaded service result is claimed. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [private sort-key migration v1](docs/experiments/mmm-sort-key-migration-v1-results.md)
passes a finite ALTER/backfill/reopen feasibility probe and full-scan
readiness negatives for absent, partial and corrupt keys. Three EventFrame
payloads/vectors and ingestion motion survive; exact-LSN SQL excludes
future `.125Z` at as-of `.120Z`. LibraVDB record versions advance1->2
while eventframed runtime version stays fixed, and the ordinary snapshot
compatibility guard still accepts the pre-migration snapshot. The publication
prototype above addresses only a private close/reopen marker; writer fencing,
crash recovery and loaded service still precede serving cutover. Goal 6 and
all seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [rolling-LSN sortable-key v3](docs/experiments/mmm-rolling-lsn-reader-v3-results.md)
passes a frozen **test-only backend component** on subsecond as-of data
that defeated the old string predicate. Three fresh rotated-pair runs
have pooled read-call p99 ratios 0.976/0.864/0.862, writer ratios
0.994/1.002/1.000 and candidate post-ack lag p99
14.927/4.632/4.682 ms, with 576 correct reads and 256 writes per arm.
All 472 row keys match EventFrame time; reopen, exact-retry and fail-closed
partial-row controls pass. A dedicated concurrency race run passes.
Existing-collection migration, cross-DB authority, learner freshness and
full Recall service are untested. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [fixed-width UTC sort key v1](docs/experiments/mmm-lsn-fixed-key-v1-results.md)
passes a **raw private-store** fractional as-of contract probe: the new
nine-digit `available_at_sort` field returns `{early}`, `{early}` and
`{early, late}` at `.1/.12/.15Z`, including after reopen. The key is not
yet generated by eventframed's versioned writer, old rows are not
migrated, and no loaded latency is measured. It is a correctness lead,
not a Goal 6 pass. Production untouched; all seven goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [TimeField drop-in probe](docs/experiments/mmm-lsn-timefield-v1-results.md)
**FAILS** the fractional as-of falsifier despite successful insert/bind:
declaring the unchanged RFC3339Nano metadata as LibraVDB `TimeField`
still returns the same future leak and omissions as `StringField`. A
separate validated sortable availability key and existing-row migration
are needed before the rolling-LSN timing opportunity can be considered
as-of safe. All seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [fractional as-of falsifier](docs/experiments/mmm-lsn-fractional-availability-v1-results.md)
**DISQUALIFIES** the current string-filtered rolling-LSN design for general
Goal 6 serving. At as-of `.1Z`, exact-LSN SQL admits an event available at
`.15Z`; at `.12Z` and `.15Z`, it omits a past `.1Z` event. Ordinary
`Store.Search` returns the correct sets. Variable-width `available_at`
strings do not preserve time order under lexical SQL comparison. This is
a confirmed future-data leak in the research candidate, not production.
All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [rolling-LSN captured-view v2](docs/experiments/mmm-rolling-lsn-reader-v2-results.md)
passes its frozen **whole-second timing fixture** twice after an offer-time
lag correction: pooled read-call p99 ratios 0.891 and 0.902, writer ratios
0.995 and 0.997, post-ack lag p99 15.564 and 14.881 ms. The fractional
falsifier above overrides any as-of-safety inference from those timing
passes. No loaded service, migration or Goal 6 completion is claimed.

2026-10-01 [LSN availability-schema v1](docs/experiments/mmm-lsn-availability-schema-v1-results.md)
passes a private, test-only Goal 6 contract probe. Declaring
`available_at: StringField` lets parameterized exact-LSN SQL return
`{past}` at old LSN20 and `{past, visible}` at new LSN24 while excluding
the future event; reopen and race checks pass. This identifies the v1
rolling SQL binder failure as a missing schema declaration, not an
unsupported predicate. Existing-collection migration, concurrent visible
writes, service latency and freshness remain untested. All seven whole
goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [rolling-LSN visible-write v1](docs/experiments/mmm-rolling-lsn-reader-v1-results.md)
cannot complete its frozen Goal 6 comparison. The current-path control
exhausted four post-read publication attempts while visible batches advanced
versions 408->424->440->456->472. Candidate-only warm-up independently
failed because the current LibraVDB collection has no SQL-bound
`available_at` field, so an exact-LSN query cannot express the required
as-of predicate. No rolling-path timing or freshness claim follows. A new
design needs schema-backed availability plus a frozen coherent read
linearization contract. All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production
untouched.

2026-10-01 [pinned-LSN reader v1](docs/experiments/mmm-pinned-lsn-reader-v1-results.md)
passes a frozen **future-only** Goal 6 component screen in three rotated pairs:
576 correct reads/arm, pooled read-call p99 40.153->27.521 ms (0.685x),
summed writer-completion ratio 0.958, and no leaked temporal lease. A
new as-of-visible write correctly invalidates the captured publication guard
even while pinned SQL returns the historical view. This does **not** serve
immediate new events; a rolling fresh-LSN design and loaded service test remain.
All seven whole goals remain OPEN, and production is untouched.

2026-10-01 [batch cap v2 design screen](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-cap-v2-results.md)
**FAILS** both cap-4 and cap-8 on the unchanged Goal 6 reader guard.
Three rotated fresh triples retain all 256 acknowledgements, 192
as-of-correct searches and per-version motion. Pooled search-call p99
ratios versus single control are 1.438 for cap-4 and 1.806 for cap-8,
above the <=1.10 ceiling, despite writer-completion ratios 0.277 and
0.153. No candidate advances to confirmation. Further cap tuning on
these cohorts is not justified; seek a distinct reader/writer isolation
design. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [batch reader-factor diagnostic v1](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-reader-factor-v1-results.md)
separates static future-corpus growth from concurrent writer effects for
Goal 6. In three rotated 200-past/256-future/192-search blocks, pooled
search p99 was 2.817 ms base static, 2.983 ms expanded same-tenant static,
9.786 ms with concurrent *other-tenant* batch writes, and 20.374 ms with
concurrent same-tenant writes. All searches returned exactly the 200
past-eligible records; motion and future-only as-of checks passed. The
global `eventMu` read/write exclusion explains a cross-tenant contention
path; the extra same-tenant tail is not yet decomposed. This is not a
reader-safe batch rescue or a full service test. Goal 6 and all seven whole
goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [batch acknowledgement load v1](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-ack-load-v1-results.md)
**FAILS** its frozen Goal 6 backend contention screen. Three rotated
200-base/256-future-write/192-search pairs retained all per-event
acknowledgements, motion and as-of filtering. Batch-16 reduced pooled
offer-to-ack p99 999.865->42.722 ms and writer completion ratio to0.088,
but search-call p99 rose12.198->18.755 ms (1.538x), above the 1.10x
reader ceiling. Single-write control backpressured nominal 1 ms offers
to ~11 ms actual median gaps, while candidate sustained ~1 ms; this is
not a matched-realized-rate service comparison. Opt-in race load completed
without a race report but failed the same reader gate. Do not wire batch-16
as-is; full Goal 6 and all seven whole goals remain OPEN. Production untouched.

2026-10-01 [post-contract batch conversion parity v1](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-conversion-parity-v1-results.md)
passes a test-only four-turn `CaptureTurn` control versus real-store
batch-intent comparison: matching durable EventFrames, raw-content metadata,
vectors and +4 runtime-version deltas before and after reopen; exact raw-turn
digest retry and changed-turn conflict also pass. Full-text embedding and
EventFrame-digest shortcuts are discriminating negative controls. Focused
repeat, full package race and vet pass. No queue, external index, mixed
`Observe` traffic, loaded serving latency or 4 ms freshness was tested.
Goal 6 and all seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [real-store batch intent prototype v1](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-intent-prototype-v1-results.md)
passes finite test-only crash/reopen, exact retry, mixed duplicate/new,
per-version motion, future-only/backfill as-of, and fail-closed mismatch
controls against raw LibraVDB plus a private SQLite sidecar. Two regression
tests exposed and fixed monotonic-clock retry and sidecar-only duplicate
authority; missing-sidecar recreation over existing history is rejected.
Matched 128-event medians are 872.31 ms for protected singles and 70.69 ms
for protected batch-16 (12.34x isolated throughput), with 11.1% overhead
over raw batch-16. Race and vet pass. This does not test concurrent owners,
hardware power loss, conversion parity, or loaded 4 ms freshness. Goal 6 and
all seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [batch motion authority v1](docs/experiments/mmm-batch-motion-model-v1-results.md)
passes13/13 finite isolated controls for per-event version, motion and
touch assignment across a batch, duplicate-only clean abort, as-of
reads, crash phases and uncertain-commit quarantine. A bridge Go test
passes against real `store.ResearchSnapshotCompatible`; package tests,
vet and exact model replay pass. Two initial model artifacts were
superseded after protocol and atomicity audit corrections, and remain
retained. This does not test real SQLite/LibraVDB cross-DB recovery or
the 4 ms loaded service. Goal 6 and all seven whole goals remain OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [event-batch authority audit](docs/experiments/mmm-event-batch-authority-v1.md)
confirms a strong *isolated* backend opportunity for Goal 6: in the
existing 128-event benchmark, batch-16 writes take62.9 ms versus841.8 ms
for singles, consistent with the earlier 200-event index-scaling study.
Directly wiring that raw batch would violate the publication and durable
lineage ingestion contracts, which require exactly one version and one
motion entry per call; raw batch advances up to16 versions. A safe rescue
needs ordered per-event motion/touch publication, uncertain-commit handling
and loaded per-event writer/learner latency. This is not a Goal 6 pass;
production untouched and all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [conditional gate transport v1](docs/experiments/mmm-conditional-transport-v1-results.md)
rejects direct reuse of the v2 two-cell Hoeffding rule at MMM-style
online audit volume. On 16,000 fresh trajectories, stable and common
false flags are zero, but clock-256 live shifts flag 0/1,000 in every
split/schedule; start shifts flag only 1/1,000 on each immediate split.
The unchanged radius needs an empirical gap ~0.718 at ~64 online
labels/side/context, while the full population effect is 0.45 and
late-shift cumulative dilution is ~0.225. Independent row audit and
byte-exact replay pass. This is a projected transport diagnostic, not
actual MMM integration, and it does not invalidate stronger sequential
tests or a properly costed reference bank. Goal 3 and all seven whole
goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [Kalman delayed-onset information audit](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-onset-information-v1.md)
establishes a matched-world limit for Goal 2. Across 32 paired streams,
all 512 first-16 post-change forecasts are identical even though 257
contexts have different outcome rules; no changed label can arrive before
clock 272. A later distinguishing audited packet makes the filters diverge
in every stream. Under an equal prior over these two worlds, the first-16
population excess Brier versus a world-aware oracle is at least 0.10125.
This is a diagnostic, not a Kalman rescue, and it does not rule out improved
recovery after feedback or a separately measured pre-label sensor. All
seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [unchanged deadline replication v39](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-deadline-replication-v39-results.md)
confirms v38's two-sided Goal 6 failure on four fresh matched
4 ms pairs. Every deadline candidate trial keeps frontier
age p99 220–225 ms, with64 labels/256 writes/zero drops and
durable/as-of checks, but **every** matched writer-p99 ratio
exceeds1.25 (1.293,1.311,1.466,1.383); pooled ratio is1.332.
Recall and writer total-completion gates pass. Retiring the
current30/200 ms deadline pair avoids tuning on consumed
timing cohorts. Pursue a guarded-write throughput or proved
time-partitioned-authority design; all seven whole goals OPEN,
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [deadline-aware scheduling v38](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-deadline-gate-v38-results.md)
is a Goal 6 near miss, not a pass. On fresh 4 ms matched pairs,
candidate frontier age p99 reaches220.90 ms (<250), Recall
p99 ratio0.792 and writer starts70/80 control; all 256 writes
and64 labels finish with zero drops and as-of/durable checks.
But writer offer p99 rises24.99->31.95 ms (1.279x), exceeding
the frozen1.25x allowance. Do not retune the30/200 ms
deadlines on this cohort. Next explore a true guarded-write
throughput improvement or time-partitioned authority with
formal no-future proof; all seven whole goals OPEN,
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [admission-priority scheduling v37](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-priority-gate-v37-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal 6 two-sided screen. At 4 ms offers,
pooled frontier-to-published p99 improves from~430 ms control
to324.34 ms candidate but still exceeds250 ms; writer
offer p99 rises25.38->36.23 ms (1.428x) beyond1.25x.
Recall p99, writer total completion and writer-start overlap
pass their gates; all 256 writes/64 labels finish with zero
drops and as-of/durable/replay checks. Do not loosen the
writer or freshness thresholds. Test a deadline-aware fair
scheduler on fresh fixtures or find a true guarded-write
throughput improvement. All seven whole goals OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [future-writer factor v36](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-writer-factor-v36-results.md)
strongly attributes the 4 ms Goal 6 freshness failure to
contention from concurrent future-only writes at the shared
guard. With 256 writes, guard-entry p50 is14.96 ms and
frontier age p99 429.13 ms; without that writer, guard entry
p50 is<1 us and frontier age p99 5.46 ms, with the same 192
Recalls/64 labels/zero drops. Removing writes is not an
acceptable rescue. A scheduling or batching candidate must
preserve write throughput/latency, as-of authority and durable
learning while clearing the 4 ms freshness gate. All seven
whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [guard-entry attribution v35](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-guard-entry-v35-results.md)
finds the main serial admission cost before candidate validation:
at 4 ms offers, guard entry/compatibility p50/p99 is14.51/27.01 ms,
while actual candidate validation is0.87/1.87 ms. Frontier age
p99 is418.72 ms despite zero drops and serving p99 45.91 ms.
Concurrent future-write contention is plausible but not yet
proven; run a writer-on/off control before changing the guard.
All seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [admission guard sub-operation v34](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-admission-guard-v34-results.md)
falsifies durable append/readback or second-validation as the
4 ms Goal 6 bottleneck. In fresh 6/4 ms fixtures, the
pre-callback guard-entry/source-validation median is15.71/16.81 ms;
durable admit is0.15/0.14 ms, readback0.03/0.02 ms and
post-record validation0.92/0.82 ms. The pre-callback bucket
still mixes guard acquisition and first source validation.
Split those next; do not delete authority checks based on this
profile. All seven whole goals OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [admission-wait attribution v33](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-admission-wait-v33-results.md)
locates the 4 ms Goal 6 backlog in the bounded selected handoff:
median/p99 wait266.77/381.89 ms, while committed-journal lookup
is1.05/2.22 ms, receipt-to-admission is microseconds, and guarded
admission is17.98/25.24 ms. The oldest labels each wait373–382 ms
in handoff. This falsifies an ordering-map or journal-lookup
bottleneck; serial admission appears slower than the ~12 ms
selected-arrival interval. Profile the guard's sub-operations before
altering validation or durability. All seven whole goals OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [pre-feedback attribution v32](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-pre-feedback-v32-results.md)
falsifies the prior feedback-queue explanation for the 4 ms Goal 6
freshness failure. On a fresh 4 ms fixture, frontier age p99 is
423.19 ms; the five oldest labels each wait383–394 ms before
guarded admission starts, spend13–15 ms in that admission, and
under1 ms between admission end and feedback offer. At 6 ms,
frontier age p99 is57.80 ms and median guarded admission16.46 ms.
The pre-admission bucket still mixes journal lookup, selected
handoff, ordering and serial backlog; split it before a rescue.
All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [cadence confirmation/phase trace v31](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-cadence-v31-results.md)
confirms a finite 6 ms (~167 offers/s) Goal 6 component across
three fresh matched pairs: pooled on offer p99 38.72 ms,
frontier-to-published p99 75.09 ms, feedback-to-published p99
26.06 ms, 64 labels and zero drops per enabled trial. A new
4 ms diagnostic repeats the freshness failure at417.66 ms with
zero drops; the five oldest labels spend388–402 ms between tap
take and feedback offer, not in tap drainage or publication.
That interval mixes ordered admission and prior-label waiting;
attribute it before a rescue. All seven whole goals remain OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [full-Recall cadence screen v30](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-cadence-v30-results.md)
finds a Goal 6 **freshness** boundary with unchanged v29 learner:
one matched pair each at measured 8/6/4 ms offers. 8 and 6 ms pass
finite serving/freshness/durable gates; 4 ms keeps on offer p99
42.09 ms, ratio1.093 and zero drops but frontier-to-published p99
reaches401.49 ms against250 ms. Feedback-to-published is26.05 ms,
so phase attribution should focus before feedback. A closed-tap
negative control fails closed. Next repeat 6 ms and profile 4 ms;
do not retune the 4 ms cohort. All seven whole goals remain OPEN.

2026-10-01 [decoupled live-learning drainage v29](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-decoupled-drain-v29-results.md)
PASSES the frozen finite Goal 6 full-Recall component and the focused
race lifecycle check (three race runs). Three ordinary matched pairs
keep 192 frontiers, 64 durable labels and zero drops per enabled trial;
pooled on offer p99 is35.34 ms, frontier-to-published p99 41.37 ms,
and feedback-to-published p99 21.63 ms. A bounded capacity-32 drainer
channel separates tap intake from event-time-ordered guarded admission,
addressing the v28 race-stressed loss without backdating or an
unbounded queue. Next freeze burst/rate and missing-frontier tests,
then cross-epoch/power-loss and real-agent labels. This is not whole
Goal 6 completion; all seven whole goals remain OPEN. Production untouched.

2026-10-01 [ordered live-learning pipeline v28](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-ordered-pipeline-v28-results.md)
PASSES the frozen ordinary finite Goal 6 component (three matched
off/on trials; on offer p99 36.87 ms, frontier-to-published p99 42.93 ms,
feedback-to-published p99 20.51 ms pooled; 64 labels and zero drops per
enabled trial), but FAILS the focused `-race` lifecycle check after
55 tap drops and only 35 labels. The ordering removed the observed
backdating failure in this run, but the single consumer still couples
tap drainage to slow guarded admission. Next isolate drainage with a
separate bounded stage and retain zero-drop/as-of/durable checks.
Goal 6 and all other whole goals remain OPEN. Production untouched.

2026-10-01 [live-learning phase v27](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-live-phases-v27-results.md)
localizes v26's Goal 6 freshness failure. Across192 selected labels in
three ordinary full-load trials, tap enqueue-to-take p99 is418.23 ms of
456.67 ms full frontier-to-publication p99; guarded feedback and worker
publication are much smaller. Per-label phase conservation passes. A
focused `-race` replay explicitly shows out-of-order event time: the third
frontier is at00:00:12 after a label already available at00:00:15, so
the no-backdating guard correctly rejects it. Next test a bounded
event-time ordering policy and a throughput rescue together; queue
relocation or clock relabeling is not a rescue. Goal 6 remains OPEN.

2026-10-01 [eight-worker durable live learning v26](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-live-learning-v26-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal 6 integration screen. Three matched off/on trials
with 192 full 200-event Recalls, 256 future-only writes, queue64 and
64 bound durable labels per enabled trial keep on-arm offer p99 at34.98 ms
(pooled), but frontier-to-published p99 is410.82 ms against a250 ms gate;
feedback-offer-to-published p99 is21.04 ms. All ordinary as-of, journal
reopen, 128-row ledger/replay, no-drop and visible-mutation rejection
checks pass. A focused `-race` run instead rejects the third label as
backdated after out-of-order committed-frontier delivery (leading
explanation, not yet separately traced) and then drops work. Preserve both
negatives. Next instrument tap-wait versus admission, and test a bounded
event-time ordering policy without allowing future feedback into earlier
forecasts. No production change or whole-goal completion follows.

2026-10-01 [eight-worker full Recall v25](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-workers-v25-results.md)
PASSES a frozen Goal 6 **latency component** on the unchanged guarded
SQLite WAL/FULL research path: at a measured ~125 offers/s, eight workers
give pooled loaded offer p99 48.13 ms (trial p99s 48.70/45.18/57.45 ms),
versus 130.81 ms with four workers; quiet p99 is9.35 ms. Sixteen workers
also pass loaded p99 but offer no clear gain and have one noisier quiet
trial. Exact 200-event nomination/as-of, future-only writes, durable
reopen, focused eight-worker race and single-journal contract race pass.
This does not test live-label freshness, cross-epoch mutation/recovery,
rate bursts, or real-agent benefit; it is not Goal 6 completion and is not
enabled in production. Next freeze a burst/freshness/mutation matrix.

2026-10-01 [full Recall capacity v24](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-capacity-v24-results.md)
adds a causal constraint for Goal 6: under the 200-event/four-worker/8 ms
offer fixture with concurrent writes, mean occupied call times are
33.36-33.57 ms (single SQLite), 42.35-43.77 ms (8 ms group), and
54.98-56.63 ms (1 ns group), all above the nominal 32 ms/worker budget.
Completion-order queue medians rise in each of three rotated trials.
Single-journal mean guard wait is11.59-11.77 ms versus0.774-0.785 ms for
SQLite insert/commit. Actual offer jitter and group internal phases remain
unmeasured, so this is a nominal capacity diagnosis, not a root-cause proof
or a Goal 6 pass. Full v24 `-race` replay passes separately (473.144 s).
The next test should record offer timing and vary bounded
worker count or publication architecture while keeping durability/as-of.

2026-10-01 [group-journal dwell v23](docs/experiments/mmm-recall-dwell-v23-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal 6 loaded-latency screen. With the same 200-event,
four-worker, WAL/FULL, exact-as-of full Recall fixture, 1 ns group dwell
cuts quiet p99 but worsens writer offer p99 from 570.61 ms (8 ms dwell) to
1175.76 ms; single guarded SQLite is 202.07 ms. The no-wait arm mostly
forms one-entry batches and accumulates 1101.55 ms queue p99. Three rotated
trials, 576 offers/case/arm, 768 writes/writer-arm, durable reopen, exact
nomination and no-future checks pass; focused race and vet pass. Dwell alone
is not the loaded bottleneck. Next investigate publication/commit ordering
or admission capacity without weakening as-of/durable-acknowledgement rules.
No Goal 6 completion or production change follows.

Kalman evidence correction: the already-run [v1](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-window-v1-results.md)
and [v2](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-early-v2-results.md) component studies
show cheap late linear-shift tracking but fail Goal 2/4 early-recovery,
stationary-protection, and nonlinear gates. The new
[post-hoc phase profile](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-phase-profile-v1.md)
locates the delayed failure: no post-change audited label arrives in the
first 16 clocks, and raising process noise does not protect those forecasts.
Do not repeat a process-noise-only sweep. A future Kalman candidate needs a
separately justified onset cue, change-hazard model, or uncertainty-aware
fallback, with fresh frozen cohorts and equal evidence/cost accounting. This
diagnostic is not a new confirmation or a whole-goal success.

2026-10-01 [evidence-responsive observation budget](docs/experiments/mmm-observation-responsive-v1-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal1/7 joint screen on fresh off-boundary shifts. A
working 8-start x10-alternative likelihood-ratio monitor alerts after
arrived labels and improves confirmation late360/late400 post expected
Brier .2392->.2261/.3072->.2822, but median alert delay61-66 clocks
leaves only5.4/2.3 recovery-clock gains, below the frozen10-clock floor.
Noisy/delayed protection and delayed arrived-label parity also fail.
Stable false alerts are2/256 design and1/256 confirmation, empirically,
not a certified 1% bound for the fitted baseline. Independent monitor/
score chronology audit and exact full `-race` replay pass; isolated
monitor-plus-update p99 is2.50-8.17us. No retuning on these cohorts.
Production/paper untouched; all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [finite-horizon observation reserve](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-reserve-v1-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal1/7 joint screen on fresh design and confirmation
streams. Predictably reserving48 of128 label requests for clocks384..511
improves confirmation late-bit2 post expected Brier .2798->.2025, restricted
mean recovery119.9->100.0 clocks, and misses .543->.020. Yet it worsens
early, noisy, delayed, skewed and out-of-family cases; stable stays flat.
The late change is structurally aligned to the declared stage boundary,
not detected online. Independent per-tick audit and byte-identical full
`-race` replay pass; unchanged component update p99 is1.58-5.88us.
No ratio tuning on these cohorts. Production/paper untouched; all seven
whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [known-hypothesis acquisition stress transfer](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-transfer-v1-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal1/7 overall screen on independent generators and fits,
despite strong post expected-Brier gains in4/5 known cases on design and5/5
on confirmation, plus stable and OOD relative non-harm. The late bit2
change at clock384 reduces recovery misses from~.70-.78 to~.47-.54 but
restricted mean recovery improves only~5 clocks, below the frozen10-clock
lead. Every arm requests128 labels; 3,584 trajectories independently audit
and confirmation replay is byte-identical. Component update p99 is1.54-5.21us,
not serving latency. The result strengthens score-transfer evidence without
establishing timely late recovery or any whole goal. Production and paper
untouched; all seven goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [learned-contrast v4 gated-null rescue](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-v4-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal1/7 component screen on new design and untouched
confirmation streams. The exact v2 selector preserves all 128 requested
clocks per matched arm, so forecast gating is isolated. Null full Brier
improves .38146->.29461 on confirmation but misses the .27 cap; immediate
bit2 recovery slows 129.11->136.31 clocks, delayed 153.55->159.05, and
majority OOD post expected Brier worsens .29613->.29683. Stable behavior
is unchanged. Independent per-tick verification, source hashes and exact
confirmation replay pass. Component forecast/select p99 is sub-microsecond,
but paired update p99 varied 3.29-10.71 us and once missed the 10 us cap.
No threshold retuning on these cohorts. All seven whole goals OPEN;
production and whitepaper untouched.

2026-10-01 [v3 null-model/schedule factorial](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-v3-factorial-results.md)
separates the failed v3 rescue on its consumed tapes without claiming fresh
validation. On a fixed old acquisition schedule, null-model mass adds
14.74/12.23 clocks of confirmation bit2 recovery delay immediate/delayed;
changing only the schedule under the old model adds6.15/0.36. For average
immediate-bit2 expected Brier, schedule contributes more harm (.00763) than
model substitution (.00390), so neither mechanism can be ignored. The null
model still improves null expected Brier .12820 and majority .01672 on the
same evidence schedule. Both diagonal replays are exact at every clock,
and race replay is byte-identical. This is a retrospective lead for a newly
frozen evidence-gated null forecast, not a Goal7 completion. Production
untouched; all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [learned-contrast v3 null/unknown transfer](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-v3-results.md)
FAILS the frozen joint Goal1/7 component screen on independent design and
confirmation streams. Adding a Bernoulli(.5) null branch cuts confirmation
null full Brier .37991->.26496 and improves out-of-family majority post
expected Brier .29737->.28023, but 255/256 majority streams still miss the
recovery threshold. Known bit2 recovery slows from130.32 to145.69 clocks
immediate and156.70 to168.77 delayed; the new selector beats its own
controls by only1-5 clocks, below the frozen10-clock floor. Known bit0
protection also fails. All 3,584 trajectories and 11,010,048 forecasts
pass independent reconstruction; confirmation replay is byte-identical.
Isolated cost and package tests pass. Preserve both benefit and harm; no
retuning on these cohorts. Production untouched, all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [learned-contrast acquisition v2](docs/experiments/mmm-learned-contrast-v2-results.md)
PASSES a frozen known-hypothesis Goal7 component screen on independent design
and confirmation cohorts. An alternative chosen from an eleven-rule family
using arrived labels lowers confirmation bit2 post256 Brier .18595/.18174
random/uncertainty to .16208, and delayed bit2 .21945/.21904 to .20380,
at exactly128 requests and with positive fit-cluster lower gain endpoints.
The component also passes bit0/interaction non-harm and stable/null relative
gates, but null absolute Brier is ~.38 because there is no unknown/null rule,
and delayed first64 recovery is almost unchanged. This is not automatic
hypothesis invention, an Anti-Pigeon certificate, real-agent validation or a
whole Goal7 completion. Production untouched; all seven whole goals OPEN.

2026-10-01 [Kalman early-recovery v2](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-early-v2-results.md)
FAILS its frozen Goal2/4 component screen on new design and confirmation
fits/streams. Increasing fixed process noise from .0005 to .01 improves
confirmation abrupt-shift first64 Brier .30073->.23935, but delayed first64
is still .34324 versus .25153 for last-64; stable full-stream harm is .02174
and nonlinear last128 harm is .11693 versus last-64. The smaller .002 noise
also fails. All 320 trajectories pass independent score/delivery auditing;
confirmation replay is exact apart from timing. Do not retune noise on these
cohorts or promote the filter. All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production
untouched.

2026-10-01 [MMM contrast acquisition v1](docs/experiments/mmm-contrast-acquisition-v1-results.md)
FAILS its frozen overall screen on independent design/confirmation
streams. With equal128 nominations,256 label requests and512 reference
context proposals, the declared-hypothesis contrast selector flags
996/1,000 member shifts versus639 random and627 uncertainty, and lowers
first-64 Brier by0.03701/0.05296. Post-256 Brier gain is only
0.01366 versus random and0.01998 versus uncertainty, below the frozen
0.02 magnitude floor; design also misses one arrived-label parity
screen. The alternative rule is oracle-supplied, not learned, and the
gate does not drive a split. All seven whole goals remain OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [MMM paired-evidence transfer v1](docs/experiments/mmm-member-pair-feasibility-v1-results.md)
FAILS its frozen transfer screen on independent design/confirmation streams
using the existing MMM member label generator. The unchanged v4 paired
gate flags only5/1,000 passive and108/1,000 targeted clock-256 member
shifts in confirmation, versus the declared800/1,000 targeted floor.
Targeted same-bit acquisition supplies about78 complete pairs for about
256 reference-context proposals and198-199 observed labels over512
clocks; common-shift/null false flags remain0. The conditional member
gap here is0.45, not v4's0.80. No actual member-state, scored-law or
serving conclusion follows. All seven whole goals remain OPEN;
production untouched.

2026-10-01 [paired betting certificate v4](docs/experiments/mmm-paired-bet-v4-results.md)
PASSES a frozen fixed-model component screen on independent design and
confirmation splits. On the same sparse-delayed matched-pair budget where

betting mean flags936/1,000 live-only and928/1,000 reference-only
clock-256 shifts, with0 prechange flags,4/1,000 boundary-null flags and
0/1,000 common-co-drift flags. Median sparse flag clock435 leaves a

2026-10-01 [paired current-context v3](docs/experiments/mmm-paired-context-v3-results.md)
FAILS its frozen full screen on fresh design and confirmation seeds. A
128-clock paired conditional certificate distinguishes member-only shifts
(1,000/1,000 detections each, median clock361 after change at256) from
common co-drift (0/1,000 false splits) when all same-context pairs are
available. Under25% nomination,20% per-member missingness and0..31 delay,
it flags0/1,000 for every shift case: about79 usable pairs over512 clocks
cannot power the fixed finite-horizon radius in a128-clock window. The

2026-10-01 [conditional window power v2](docs/experiments/mmm-conditional-window-v2-results.md)
finds a narrow fresh-seed rescue for Goal3's audit certificate: with a
512-label precollected reference, a fixed 256-clock live window flags
997/1,000 immediate and917/1,000 sparse-delayed swaps beginning at clock256,
versus6/1,000 for the paired cumulative control in each. Stable flags are
0/1,000, but median detection clocks463/479 are late. With only online
reference audits, the window flags460/1,000 immediate and54/1,000 delayed
late swaps, and also loses power on swaps from clock0. The frozen full
transfer screen FAILS. The hidden-change reset is a diagnostic, not a

scored-law benefit, multi-bucket certificate or serving latency is proven.
All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production untouched.

2026-10-01 [conditional evidence power v1](docs/experiments/mmm-conditional-power-v1-results.md)
FAILS a fresh 24,000-trajectory transfer screen. With an independently
precollected 512-label reference, the fixed two-cell gate confirms swaps
present from clock0 in1,000/1,000 trajectories, but flags only16/1,000
immediate and1/1,000 sparse-delayed swaps introduced at clock256. With
online reference audits it flags0/1,000 late swaps in both schedules, and
the sparse-delayed start-swap rate is778/1,000 below the frozen800 floor.
All four stable cells show0/1,000 false flags. This exposes cumulative

2026-10-01 [Kalman fallback retrospective](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-fallback-retrospective.md)
rejects two cheap rescues on consumed v1 streams without spending a fresh
confirmation cohort. The existing four-expert log mixture still gives
confirmation first-64 Brier .33424 versus .25150 for last-64 on abrupt shift,
and .37211 versus .25151 with delayed feedback. A hedged-base/CUSUM fallback
improves abrupt first-64 to .24939 but triggers in13/32 stationary streams
and worsens stationary full Brier .06224->.07779; delayed first-64 remains
.29652. This is post-hoc diagnostic evidence, not validation. Do not retune
on v1 confirmation. All seven whole goals remain OPEN; production unchanged.

2026-10-01 [Kalman residual challenger v1](docs/experiments/mmm-kalman-window-v1-results.md)
passes a frozen late-score/isolated-cost screen on independent design and
confirmation synthetic streams, but not the full Goal 2/4 criteria. The
11-coordinate working filter reaches confirmation last-128 Brier .05955 on
`shift128` versus .23555 for the adaptive window, with 1,056-byte state and
isolated update p99 below 1 us. Yet its first-64 Brier is .30030 versus
.25135 for the better window, delayed first-64 deficit is .11363, and the
nonlinear interaction tail loses .06161 Brier. Stable full-stream harm upper
endpoint .00943 barely clears the .01 screen. The original gate was too weak
to establish faster recovery; preserve this negative result, keep production
untouched, and require a fresh early-recovery/strong-incumbent gate before
considering integration. All seven whole goals remain OPEN.

2026-10-01 [conditional audit gate component](docs/experiments/mmm-conditional-gate-v2-results.md)
tests a fixed two-context Anti-Pigeon identifiability gap. Under a conditional
law swap with mean contextual TV0.8, the scalar correctness stream has exactly
the same Bernoulli(1/2) law before/after. A predeclared, finite-horizon
contextual Hoeffding audit flags1,000/1,000 swaps and0/1,000 stationary streams
under both complete and25%-audit/20%-missing/delayed schedules; scalar flags0.
On independent fresh seeds, authorization enables an already-arrived-label
conditional forecaster: realized Brier gain is.592487 complete and.400412
sparse delayed, with zero observed stable harm at equal audit cost. All4,000
trial rows per study independently verify and replay. This is a toy component,
not the actual MMM gate, unknown target-law certificate, learned contexts or
general agent result. Goal3 and all other whole goals remain OPEN; no
production or paper change.

2026-10-01 [prepared activation feasibility](docs/experiments/mmm-prepared-activation-v1-report.md)
tests a research-only two-phase journal after the guard-contention diagnosis.
The bounded model has zero invalid schedules for guarded version/ID validation
and atomic acceptance, while early release and missing ID checks fail. An
identity-restored SQLite WAL/FULL prototype verifies 48,000 exact originals
after reopen but does not rescue cost: at 200 records mean guarded time changes
2.671->2.449 ms while mean total write time worsens 2.671->3.845 ms. This is
exploratory, without a predeclared timing gate, real service writes, worker
forecasts or power-loss recovery. Do not promote; production unchanged and
Goal6 OPEN.

2026-10-01 [untouched broad-location USGS retrieval](research/public-task-pilot/USGS_BROAD_CONFIRMATION_RESULTS.md)

All24 source place strings are unique, so the natural/UTC+11 wordings do not

2026-10-01 [Tadine current-contract diagnostic](research/public-task-pilot/USGS_TADINE_CURRENT_RESULTS.md)

2026-10-01 [relation-witness component](research/originbinding/RELATION_WITNESS_RESULTS.md)
