adapters within one controlled run before attributing the difference or tuning.

A [core-method publication adapter](docs/experiments/mmm-publication-store-v1.md)
now wraps all classified core mutation paths in separate research code. Coverage,
per-writer panic quarantine and memory/persistent transition tests pass three race
runs. Successful certificate/composition/maintenance parity and optional vector
forwarding now also pass on memory and persistent stores. It is not configured
in the daemon. Posterior/snap/agency success paths, remaining optional
capabilities, duplicate recovery and load replication remain unproved; the old
ingestion-only benchmark does not automatically validate this adapter.

An [unchanged v3 replication](docs/experiments/mmm-publication-load-v3-replication.md)
again passes all six queue16 cells; longer workloads complete154-158/192 at
105-106ms age p95. Queue64 fails two cells (serving p99 and age). This strengthens
only the finite ingestion-adapter result. Full mutation/recovery coverage,
persistent feedback and real-task learning remain required; no deployment.

Test-only [publication load v3](docs/experiments/mmm-publication-load-v3-results.md)
provides a bounded rescue: queue16 passes all six finite cells, completing
154-155/192 long-workload frontiers at109-116ms age p95. Queue64 completes all but
fails one252.417ms age result against250ms. The adapter wraps ONLY ingestion in
this closed test; general mutation/recovery coverage and replication remain
required. This is not production-ready store integration or real-task accuracy.

A standalone [publication state model](docs/experiments/mmm-publication-model-v1.md)
passes race-tested pending/commit/quarantine/history invariants. It models
nonblocking compatibility without ignoring in-progress mutations. It is NOT
store-wired or a throughput rescue yet: every writer and ambiguous transaction
outcome needs integration coverage, and bounded history-copy cost is unmeasured.

[Buffer rescue v2](docs/experiments/mmm-learning-buffer-v2-results.md) FAILED:
queue64 completes the64-request burst but only115-118/192 on the longer workload,
with completion p95 around790-794ms versus250ms allowed. Larger buffering is not
a sustained-throughput rescue. Eighteen arms and age/count/source audits are
recorded. Retain the dependency checks and investigate a coherent publication
design before any store-lock change; no production capacity change was made.

[Snapshot/proof timing](docs/experiments/mmm-learning-proof-v1-results.md)
identifies persistent-store snapshot lock waits as the leading admission cost:
239-267ms aggregate snapshot time versus247-274ms admission; temporal proofs take
less than .5ms. All three throughput screens still fail. Preserve validation;
evaluate bounded buffering with delay/longer-load controls, or a fully audited
coherent snapshot publication design, rather than an unsafe getter shortcut.

[Learning stage diagnostics](docs/experiments/mmm-learning-stage-v1-results.md)
locate most consumer time in admission (~7.4-8.1ms/frontier), not feedback
submission (~.04ms). Durable journal reads explain only2.5-2.9% of admission;
bypassing verification is not supported. Finer dependency/lock/prediction timing
is needed before a rescue. Both instrumented runs still fail throughput, and one
instrumented pair also fails latency. Original artifacts remain unchanged.

Actual [learning load v1](docs/experiments/mmm-learning-load-v1-results.md) FAILED
all three completion gates: only33-34/64 frontiers completed despite zero errors
and on/off p99 ratios .930, .962 and1.004. Every admitted label completed; queue
drops caused the shortfall. A separate CPU profile does not establish fitting as
the bottleneck. Instrument admission/feedback/wait stages before choosing a
rescue. This is persistent serving with real refits, not validated throughput.

Opt-in [temporal feedback v2](docs/experiments/mmm-feedback-temporal-v2-results.md)
permits proved future-only ingestion while retaining rejection of backfills,
newly visible evidence and unsupported motion. Tests cover memory and persistent
stores, actual completed delayed updates, and the latest admitted query cutoff.
Default exact-snapshot behavior remains unchanged. Concurrent loaded latency and
persistent feedback state remain untested, so direction 6 is still in progress.

The [stateless public generation pilot](research/public-task-pilot/GENERATION_STATUS.md)
is prepared for 56 replay-context requests with oracle separation and grading
tests. Actual generation calls remain **zero**: the local service has only an
embedding model. A model-download or lab-endpoint choice is pending. This does
not stop other directions and is not an answer-quality result; consumed-task
replay will still not establish untouched real-agent generalization.

An explicit [feedback bridge v1](docs/experiments/mmm-feedback-bridge-v1-results.md)
now binds labels to committed journal/event pairs, rejects duplicate requests
within its bounded lifetime, and refuses changed dependency snapshots. Actual
service fixtures reach 48 labels/refits with exact synchronous parity; targeted
race tests pass three repetitions. Caller verification, persisted replay history,
temporal reuse and real-task accuracy remain open; this is not production-ready
continuous learning or evidence authentication.

Committed [frontier handoff v1](docs/experiments/mmm-frontier-handoff-v1-results.md)
connects real Service.Recall inputs to the background learner in a test harness.
All pre-packing candidates are journalled; 48 explicit fixture labels reproduce
the synchronous model and served packets remain identical. Failed journal
attempts produce no handoff. A full-queue extraction waste was fixed. This is
not real-task accuracy, persistent loaded latency, or a verified feedback bridge;
cross-request deduplication and dependency validation remain required.

Actual [background learner v1](docs/experiments/mmm-background-learner-v1-results.md)
now learns in a separate worker and publishes immutable research snapshots.
Batched synchronous parity, bounded retry/discard and concurrent-reader tests
cover the lifecycle. It is not service-wired and does not repair the failed
lexical representation. Snapshot-only scoring is about 34.4 ns with no
allocations; loaded serving latency and verified real-task feedback remain open.

Context reliability [v21](docs/experiments/mmm-context-reliability-v21-results.md)
FAILED the same consumed-data targeting screen: it warns on 10.13% of target
stops and enriches errors (14.81% versus 7.75%), but captures only 17.73%.
Unknown support is 9.22%; most missed errors are in clear contexts. Do not
promote or retune this on v19. Prioritize real-task representation and actual
learner-in-shadow integration over more warning-threshold variants.

Prequential reliability [v20](docs/experiments/mmm-calibration-v20-results.md)
FAILED its consumed-data error-targeting screen on 576 streams. Warnings cover
42.61% of target confidence stops but capture only 40.17% of errors; warned error
is 7.98% versus 8.83% nonwarned. Do not promote this class-level warning to an
observation trigger. Context-specific value-of-observation remains a separate
lead, and all seven directions remain in progress.

Mixture-aware [v19](docs/experiments/mmm-mixturestop-v19-results.md) FAILED its
gain screen on1728 streams, but yields a modest cheap majority improvement:
.08472 -> .08180 with2.42 ->2.53 reads. No harm/cost gate fails. Complete replay
passes. Confident shared mistakes remain; investigate prequential calibration
without treating this partial gain as completion or real-task evidence.

Stopping [v18](docs/experiments/mmm-stopping-disagreement-v18-results.md) finds
427/4608 majority confirmation frames with confident guide but uncertain mixture;
they account for24.1% of observation deficit. Most deficit persists when both are
confident. Test mixture-aware stopping as a partial lead, not a correctness gate
or assumed complete rescue. Calibration and forecast-quality gaps remain open.

Full-budget [v17](docs/experiments/mmm-fullbudget-v17-results.md) meets the targeted
majority gain but FAILS the overall protocol on multiplexer. Majority confirmation
Brier improves .08648 -> .08090 while reads rise2.45 ->6. Complete replay passes.
Test mixture-aware stopping to avoid unconditional extra reads; forecast quality
and real-task transfer remain separate unsolved requirements.

Known-law [v16](docs/experiments/mmm-error-budget-v16-results.md) separates errors:
majority is predominantly observation-limited; multiplexer is predominantly
forecast-limited. Confidence-stopped majority frames account for99.8% of measured
observation deficit. Test remaining-budget reads before altering models again;
this is consumed-data diagnosis, not evidence that extra reads will succeed.

Retained window [v15](docs/experiments/mmm-windowbank-v15-results.md) FAILED
on1728 streams: both bank variants meet the mean-harm allowance but miss required
gains. Multiplexer differences versus subset64 are tiny and intervals span zero.
This does not justify added fitting complexity. Separate learning/observation
error before further window variants; real-task representation remains unresolved.

Broader label-family [v14](docs/experiments/mmm-breadth-v14-results.md) FAILED
on1152 streams and six fitting groups. Adaptive subset retention has smaller
positive clustered gains but misses .005; static retention additionally harms
biased delayed-feedback cases beyond .01. Full replay passes. V13 is a bounded
success, not general robustness. Window adaptation with retained alternatives
is the next separate lead; no production promotion is justified yet.

Bayesian subset [v13](docs/experiments/mmm-subset-v13-results.md) PASSED the
576-stream finite screen for both retained variants. Correlated confirmation
adaptive Brier improves .13881 -> .11486 versus the forest. Some delayed and
interaction cases still worsen within the .01 allowance. Complete replay passes;
broader fitting independence, label families and real-task transfer remain open.

Own-path [v12](docs/experiments/mmm-alternate-v12-results.md) FAILED: the empirical
tree's clustered confirmation Brier is .19435 versus short-count .14017, despite
using6 versus4.97 coordinates. This weakens the denied-observation explanation.
Deprioritize forcing this forest; [probabilistic model averaging](research/probabilistic-challenger-lead.md)
is a separate, untested small-sample lead, not an adopted rescue.

V11 [guide reconstruction](docs/experiments/mmm-acquisition-v11-guide-diagnostic.md)
matches1,179,648 forecasts. Empirical trees guide only177/4096 clustered
confirmation frames, but their Brier on acquired fields is .17990 versus .12593
for short counts. Low authority is not by itself a bug; forcing it higher lacks
support. Alternate-path quality or a stronger challenger is the next discriminator.

Online conditional acquisition [v11](docs/experiments/mmm-acquisition-v11-results.md)
FAILED on576 paired streams. Empirical and oracle modes give only tiny clustered
shift gains; no mean-harm screen fails, but required improvements are absent.
Acquired paths rarely change in that case. Reconstruct guide selection before
concluding that acquisition opportunity, rather than model quality, is the cause.

Conditional integration [v10](docs/experiments/mmm-conditional-v10-results.md)
FAILED its advancement screen on consumed v9 masks: past-audit joint estimation
helps correlated forecasts, but confirmation shift gain is .004027, below .005.
Oracle gain is only .004272, so more accurate estimation alone is not supported
as the missing rescue. Distribution-aware acquisition remains a separate lead;
no online-policy or real-task gain has been established by this diagnostic.

Dependent-input [v9](docs/experiments/mmm-dependent-v9-results.md) FAILED the
broader transfer screen for both retained challengers. Across192 streams,
fair/biased inputs retain shift gains, but correlated inputs do not; adaptive
retention also exceeds the recurring harm limit in one design group. Complete
replay and raw metric audits pass. V8 remains a finite-generator success, not
robust validation. Conditional input integration is the next untested ablation.

## Requested starting work: recommendations 1, 2 and 4

All three have implementations and recorded experiments, not just proposals.
They remain in progress rather than broadly validated:

- **1 - Evaluation:** expanded noise, shifts, interactions and partial-observation
  controls through v5-v8, followed by public retrieval pilots. Independent
  generators and observable agent outcomes remain necessary.
- **2 - Windows:** adaptive-window variants failed the v5 screen. A bounded
  rolling window helped in v6, but replacing the incumbent failed recurring
  protection in v7. Retention is therefore part of the v8 candidate, not an
  optional optimization.
- **4 - Challenger:** retained short-window tree mixtures passed the frozen v8
  synthetic screen. This does not establish real-task transfer: direct sparse
  ranking overrides subsequently failed non-harm against semantic retrieval.

See the numbered directions below for success criteria and research sources;
the chronological checkpoints retain both positive and negative findings.

Opt-in temporal shadow [v4](docs/experiments/mmm-shadow-temporal-v4-results.md)
PASSED the bounded future-arrival persistent screen:0 request errors,254-255/256
jobs completed, paired p99 within10%. Relevant/unknown changes still reject.
This advances diagnostic lifecycle integration, not actual learner or agent
validation; default remains off and sustained backfill is unresolved.

Temporal cause isolated in [future-arrival v3](docs/experiments/mmm-shadow-future-v3-results.md):
future writes give0/1536 read errors; sustained visible backfill legitimately
invalidates journals. The future workload still FAILED one paired p99 screen.
At that checkpoint, diagnostic workers over-invalidated on global version motion.
Temporal reuse was subsequently tested in v4 above; it is not permission to
ignore decision-relevant changes.

Persistent shadow [v2](docs/experiments/mmm-shadow-persistent-v2-results.md)
FAILED zero-error screening:3 stale-journal read failures across1536 reads, with
and without shadow. Write overlap is verified; enabled p99 was48.5-61.4ms in this
small pilot. A separate cancellation-accounting bug was fixed and race-tested.
Snapshot retry starvation and actual learner integration remain unresolved.

Semantic embedding [ablation](research/public-task-pilot/SEMANTIC_RESULTS.md)
changes the adoption decision: semantic baseline5/6 paraphrase top1 versus
lexical3/6 and sparse2/6. Direct overrides FAILED non-harm and stay disabled.
Prior hash-baseline wins do not validate improvements over semantic retrieval.
Actual agent/persistent-backend evidence remains outstanding.

New-subject [Nobel transfer screen](research/public-task-pilot/NOBEL_RESULTS.md):
literal6/6 but paraphrase2/6 top1 for every arm. Sparse/lexical recover one
baseline packing miss; learned ranking is not uniquely better. Low supported-
paraphrase scores overlap absent scores, so calibrated abstention is unproven.
This advances evaluation but does not complete semantic or agent validation.

Actual pre-packing [pilot integration](research/public-task-pilot/INTEGRATED_RESULTS.md)
retains5/5 Voyager2 supports and top1 versus4/5 baseline in this run; an exact
baseline tie explains the earlier3/5. All13 nominated records reach the hook;
full-pack reference confirms every returned backend score/forecast law unchanged.
This reuses consumed tasks and is not agent or database validation.

Sparse Go checkpoint: [parity and microbenchmarks](research/public-task-pilot/GO_SCORER.md).
160 Python/Go parity rows pass; optional direct ordering is distinct from capped
deltas. Extract+score200 public candidates takes about1.4ms in isolation, with
1.1MB allocation. Full retrieval/persistence/agent load and multilingual feature
semantics remain unvalidated. Default serving is unchanged.

Sparse transfer pilot: [results](research/public-task-pilot/SPARSE_RESULTS.md).
Frozen logistic rescoring passed the small Voyager2 screen (5/5 positive top1,
Brier 0.06220), but lexical coverage tied ranking and only one confirmation
cluster was tested. This is offline packed-candidate evidence, not end-to-end
agent validation. That confirmation set is now consumed for future tuning.

Real-field preflight: [design evidence](research/public-task-pilot/DESIGN_PREFLIGHT.md)
finds nine-bit feature collisions in 6/8 answer-bearing queries despite 8/8
support survival. Retaining query-term identity removes those design collisions,
but a trained sparse-feature rescue and untouched confirmation remain necessary.

Latest integration checkpoint: [read-only pre-packing research adapter](research/public-task-pilot/RANKING_INTEGRATION.md).
Targeted race tests pass; real-task quality and persisted end-to-end latency
remain unvalidated. This checkpoint does not complete any research direction.

Updated 2026-09-12. Ranked by estimated incremental engineering, compute and
evidence cost, not guaranteed accuracy gain. Keep preserved-incumbent MMM v3 as
the research baseline. v4 disagreement-based evidence selection did not establish
added value. No experiment here is production authority.

## 1. Strengthen evaluation - cheapest

Status: IN PROGRESS. Broaden fitting seeds, noise, shift timing and interaction
structure; inject missing/delayed outcomes. Separate incumbent preservation,
mixture weighting and learner changes with matched controls. Freeze protocols
before evaluation and preserve negative results.

Success: recovery and stationary protection survive different generators and
fitting samples. No serving overhead; cost is experiments and review.
Source: Gama, Sebastiao & Rodrigues (2013),
[On Evaluating Stream Learning Algorithms](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10994-012-5320-9).

## 2. Improve challenger windows - low cost

Status: IN PROGRESS. Add one bounded adaptive-window challenger to the existing
short/long controls. Discard stale training influence, not the incumbent. Compare
against fixed windows at equal outcome volume, including stationary false cuts.

Success: faster recovery without unacceptable stationary harm. An ADWIN-inspired
prototype must not claim faithful ADWIN implementation or inherit its guarantees.
Source: Bifet & Gavalda (2007),
[Learning from Time-Changing Data with Adaptive Windowing](https://www.cs.upc.edu/~Gavalda/papers/adwin06.pdf).

## 3. Refine Anti-Pigeon's evidence gate - low compute, moderate math

Status: IN PROGRESS. Compare variance-adaptive confidence sequences under the same
false-revocation budget. Keep investigation requests separate from authority to
stop sharing; do not merely lower thresholds. Preserve the stated conditional
null, coverage boundary and independent evidence requirements.

Success: shorter member-split delay without sacrificing valid error control.
Source: Howard et al. (2021),
[Time-Uniform, Nonparametric, Nonasymptotic Confidence Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.08240).

First gate study completed: [protocol](docs/experiments/mmm-gate-v1-protocol.md),
[results](docs/experiments/mmm-gate-v1-results.md). A diversified fixed-rate wealth
mixture passed the frozen gate-level rule; the predictable adaptive-rate and
hedged variants did not. This is not yet a passed MMM sharing integration.
The grid improved restricted mean delay but increased deadline misses; that
tradeoff requires a fresh tail-reliability gate before adoption.

Fresh [tail-reliability v2](docs/experiments/mmm-gate-tail-v2-results.md) FAILED
for grid, anchored90 and anchored50 under unchanged speed criteria plus explicit
miss limits. Anchored90 protects most deadline reliability but gains only about
3.5% delay, not10%. The original gate remains unchanged. Rare-event uncertainty
and integrated sharing behavior remain necessary before any adoption.

Latest integrated coverage: [fresh member study](docs/experiments/mmm-member-coverage-v1-results.md)
passes twelve simultaneous finite-horizon false-revocation bounds at512
trajectories per case and improves member-split delay36.00%. It still fails the
unchanged post-Brier improvement gate. This supersedes the v80 sample-size
limitation only for the fixed-model generators; it does not establish broader
target-law authority, fresh-fitting-sample performance or goal completion.

The subsequent [independent-fit study](docs/experiments/mmm-member-fit-breadth-v1-results.md)
varies4096-label fitting samples independently for all5120 trajectories. Coverage
and speed survive this declared population, but post-Brier still fails. This
closes that specific fitting-sample sensitivity check, not the broader recovery,
delayed-evidence, external-authority or integration requirements.

## 4. Add a sample-efficient challenger - moderate cost

Status: IN PROGRESS. Test a small bounded online tree ensemble as a challenger,
not a replacement for the incumbent. Compare learning at the same observed-label
budget. Include interaction-heavy controls because greedy trees can fail on XOR.

Success: better shifted accuracy with measured memory/update limits. The initial
tree prototype is inspired by streaming ensembles, not a full Adaptive Random
Forest implementation or a guarantee of better generalization.
Source: Gomes et al. (2017),
[Adaptive Random Forests for Evolving Data Stream Classification](https://researchcommons.waikato.ac.nz/entities/publication/d3221dd8-3611-4e0e-8873-2a2b941bda73).

## 5. Prospective real-data validation - moderate compute, higher data cost

Status: IN PROGRESS. Use isolated agent tasks with observable outcomes; journal
forecasts before feedback and distinguish verified corrections from inferred
usefulness. Include ambiguous, absent and delayed feedback. Start outcome-design
work early: more transcripts alone do not supply missing labels.

Success: better answers/retrieval on untouched tasks. Agent-task adaptation is
ours; methodological source is
[Gama et al. (2013)](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10994-012-5320-9).

Fresh public NASA task preparation is recorded in
[pilot status](research/public-task-pilot/STATUS.md). Facts were verified and
corpus/oracle separation tested; model runs remain0. Thirteen facts/sixteen
tasks with one confirmation cluster are a wiring pilot, NOT adequate statistical
validation. The isolated harness and real-field learner adapter remain to be
connected before dispatch; production stays untouched.

## 6. Production shadow integration - high engineering cost

Status: IN PROGRESS. Bounded background jobs, immutable snapshots, cancellation,
stale-result rejection and rollback; initially no influence on served answers.
Measure full-request p95/p99 under writes, concurrency and backlog. Never infer
end-to-end performance from the small cached-prediction benchmark.

Success: results arrive before staleness without harming serving latency.
Source: Dean & Barroso (2013),
[The Tail at Scale](https://research.google/pubs/the-tail-at-scale/).

Actual service diagnostic handoff and lifecycle/load pilot implemented:
[results](docs/experiments/mmm-shadow-v1-results.md). Default disabled, no served
forecast influence. Subsequent work implements real-field learner mapping,
publication/as-of guards, replayable original-forecast records and an isolated
durable wrapper. Persistent-load results now include [v27](docs/experiments/mmm-grouped-load-v27-results.md),
but those admission fixtures have no labels, fitting or durable commits. Full
history/feedback authority, loaded durable learning, realistic agent outcomes
and production-quality lifecycle integration remain open. Early small-memory
timings and later finite admission successes do not complete this direction.
The [v9 notification repeat](docs/experiments/mmm-notification-repeat-v9-results.md)
supports queue64 completion freshness on the current wrapper under the same
finite workload, with all 50 labels processed per admitted frontier. Changed
dependencies versus v8 and absent mixed-mutation load/state recovery limit the claim.
The [mixed-mutation preflight](docs/experiments/mmm-mixed-mutation-v1-results.md)
adds real-store fail-closed/rebind coverage, but does not transfer learner state
across the bridge replacement or measure concurrent mixed-write latency.
The [v2 durable-feedback feasibility](docs/experiments/mmm-durable-bridge-feasibility-v2-results.md)
adds independently audited same-epoch labels and replay behind fresh service
guards, without making the missing cross-epoch transfer or loaded service
latency claim.
The [v2 mixed-write screen](docs/experiments/mmm-durable-mixed-load-v2-results.md)
adds finite loaded latency and durable-label correctness under future-only
concurrent writes. It exposes a substantial contention cost, but does not
measure live label-completion age or general-mutation continuity.
The [v2 live-freshness follow-up](docs/experiments/mmm-durable-live-freshness-v2-results.md)
now measures offered-label to observed published snapshot before Close and
passes its finite age gate. General visible mutations, recovery across epochs
and real agent serving remain the larger unresolved requirements.

## 7. Explicit falsification-oriented observation - most difficult

Status: IN PROGRESS. Define competing explanations and observations that discriminate
between them; begin with a bounded declared hypothesis family before automatic
generation. Maximum predictive disagreement alone was not enough in v4.

Success: faster learning than random and uncertainty sampling at equal total
acquisition cost. Known hypothesis and observation models are prerequisites, not
objects supplied automatically by the cited algorithm.
Source: Golovin, Krause & Ray (2010),
[Near-Optimal Bayesian Active Learning with Noisy Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/1010.3091).

Finite known-hypothesis study completed: [results](research/hypothesis-observation/RESULTS.md).
Target-class Gini acquisition PASSED matched-model speed criteria against random
and label uncertainty. Correlated repeated evidence remains a model-validity
failure; hypothesis construction, provenance-aware dependence and actual agent
integration remain open. This does not validate automatic scientific discovery.

Source-aware follow-up: [v2 results](research/hypothesis-observation/PROVENANCE_RESULTS.md).
Unique independent-source acquisition passed matched screening and full replay.
Spoofed independence still produces confidently wrong results, sometimes more
than naive reuse. Next: model source dependence explicitly and connect validated
provenance, not merely source IDs, before considering operational adoption.

Explicit joint source-mode follow-up [v3](research/hypothesis-observation/DEPENDENCE_RESULTS.md)
FAILED the overall screen: less false confidence on copied evidence, but harm
on noisy independent evidence. Factorized Bayes agrees with exhaustive joint
enumeration. Externally informed provenance priors and acquisition stopping are
remaining leads, not validated rescues. No universal replacement is enabled.

Disjoint synthetic signal calibration [v4](research/hypothesis-observation/INFORMED_RESULTS.md)
also FAILED the overall screen. Its noisy provenance signal cannot be assumed to
transfer across source regimes. Exact inference and complete replay checks passed;
The [v5 factorial diagnostic](research/hypothesis-observation/FACTORIAL_RESULTS.md)
now points to signal-dependent posterior weighting as the stronger lead; final
selection effects remain inconclusive. The
[v6 averaging test](research/hypothesis-observation/RELIABILITY_RESULTS.md)
partially rescues misleading signals but fails independent-stream speed protection.
The [v7 mixture-acquisition test](research/hypothesis-observation/MIXTURE_ACQUISITION_RESULTS.md)
also fails the equal-budget rescue screen. The
[v8 diagnostic](research/hypothesis-observation/LOOKAHEAD_RESULTS.md) and
[v9 fresh two-step rollout](research/hypothesis-observation/LOOKAHEAD_ROLLOUT_RESULTS.md)
find earlier learning gains on copied/mixed populations but fail the full rescue.
[V10 stopping](research/hypothesis-observation/STOPPING_RESULTS.md) saves reports
but fails accuracy protection. External omitted-influence certification and
broader observation families remain leads, not automatic guarantees. No
direction is complete from these finite provenance studies; persistent shadow
and actual-agent work remain separate required evidence.

## Current work package: 1, 2 and 4

First isolate learning from observation allocation: use equal fully observed
synthetic EventFrame inputs for every arm. This intentionally does not reproduce
the six-coordinate MMM controller. Preserve the existing complete-forecast mixer
and incumbent while comparing fixed-window, adaptive-window and tree challengers.
This stage can establish learner-level evidence, not MMM integration or real-world
readiness. Integration into adaptive partial-view MMM comes only after this test.

First pilot completed: 480 streams, three fitting seeds, ten scenarios and seven
arms. See [v5 results](docs/experiments/mmm-learners-v5-results.md) and
[verification](docs/experiments/mmm-learners-v5-audit.md). Recommendations 1, 2 and 4
remain IN PROGRESS, not production-integrated.

- 1: Broader evaluation and delayed/missing-feedback checks are implemented and
  run. Full deterministic replay passed. Independent generator and real-task
  replication remain outstanding.
- 2: Adaptive challenger implemented and tested; failed the frozen improvement
  criterion. Preserve this negative result, not a tuned replacement seed.
- 4: Bounded tree challenger implemented and tested. Early-shift Brier improved
  from 0.249634 to 0.186261, but later-shift uncertainty prevented a full pass.
  Retain as a research candidate, not a validated general rescue.

Cadence follow-up completed: [v6 results](docs/experiments/mmm-cadence-v6-results.md).
The rolling64 forest PASSED the frozen learner-level rule; the persistent
immediate and matched-cadence forests did not. This advances 1/4 and gives a
fixed-window forgetting candidate relevant to 2, but does not validate adaptive
windows. Partial-view integration, independent generator validation and real
tasks remain open. All prior v5 failures remain recorded.

Next: freeze a cadence-matched ablation (trees currently update each admitted
audit; count models refit every 16), then test partial-view MMM integration on
fresh evaluation streams. Investigate late shifts, missing feedback and XOR
weaknesses without changing v5's frozen criteria or artifacts. Prospective
real-data validation and production shadow integration remain separate stages.

## Active continuation and completion rules

The goal covers ALL seven directions, not only the cheapest synthetic successes.
No direction is exhausted merely because one prototype failed or a run was
inconclusive. A passed isolated gate is not a passed integrated forecast policy.

Next work in dependency order:

1. Cadence-matched comparison completed (v6). Partial-view follow-up
   [v7](docs/experiments/mmm-partial-v7-results.md) improved both primary shifts
   but FAILED recurring-regime protection. Retaining short-count AND tree
   challengers then PASSED the unchanged synthetic gates in
   [v8](docs/experiments/mmm-retained-v8-results.md), for adaptive and static blends.
   Independent generator and real-task validation remain; do not call the whole
   direction complete from one finite synthetic family. Late shifts and scarce
   feedback still need refinement, and adaptive blending is not uniquely validated.
2. Gate deadline-reliability replication and integration (3). Preserve mean-delay
   gains but test miss-rate noninferiority before enabling a replacement.
3. Explicit finite competing-hypothesis observation experiment (7), with random
   and uncertainty controls and equal acquisition cost. Distinguish this from
   maximum forecast disagreement already tested in v4.
4. Fresh, publicly sourced task/outcome fixtures (5). Existing public-fact
   datasets have already been exercised and cannot count as untouched new
   confirmation. Simulated conversations alone are not prospective agent tasks.
5. Isolated shadow path and load tests (6): queue bounds, version rejection,
   cancellation, no served-law changes, and request p95/p99 under write load.
   No testing in production OpenClaw.

Completion requires evidence at each direction's stated scope, or a documented
lead-exhaustion assessment naming tested alternatives and remaining barriers.
Neither universal truth learning nor arbitrary-domain success is implied.
# Latest construction component

[Atomic insertion batches](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_BATCH_RESULTS.md)
now prepare up to four events and publish after one durable transaction. Serial
graph equivalence and one/four-record abrupt recovery passed. Request collection,
individual deadline behavior and sustained-load benefit remain untested; this
does not rescue the earlier failed performance gates by itself.

[Norm reuse performance screen FAILED](research/public-task-pilot/NORM_REUSE_RESULTS.md):
distance and graph checks passed, but median preparation improved only 4.7-5.9%,
below the frozen 10% requirement. Allocation was unchanged. Keep as an unadopted
research candidate; next test bounded persistence batching rather than declaring
the sustained-load failure rescued. All seven whole directions remain open.

[Private graph sustained load FAILED](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_GRAPH_LOAD_RESULTS.md)
in both repetitions: roughly 8.8ms preparation plus 3.9ms persistence exceeds
the 10ms write arrival interval, queues exhaust deadlines, and ambiguous commit
errors trigger quarantine. All 71 acknowledgements recovered. Next leads are
exact bounded norm reuse and bounded group commit, not weakening durability or
relaxing the load gate. All seven whole directions remain open.

[Abrupt insertion recovery and shutdown](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_GRAPH_CRASH_LIFECYCLE_RESULTS.md)
passed controlled pre/post-commit process-kill tests and lease-aware close checks.
The private coordinator can now be exercised under the original offered load;
retained-byte bounds and sustained-serving evidence remain missing.

[Durable private graph coordinator](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_GRAPH_WRITER_RESULTS.md)
now integrates insertion/deletion with revision publication and bounded reader/
retired-version counts. Callback-boundary and real libravdb transaction/reopen
tests passed under race detection. Abrupt-process insertion recovery, lifecycle,
byte retention and sustained offered-load gates remain open.

[Full insertion preparation cost](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_INSERT_COST_RESULTS.md)
measured 2.72-4.81ms at N=800 and 6.86-9.48ms at N=6400, excluding persistence
and queueing. The slow case nearly consumes the 10ms arrival interval at the
original 100 writes/sec load. Durable integration must measure this boundary;
no sustained-load rescue or whole-goal completion is established.

[Composed private insertion](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_INSERT_RESULTS.md)
now matches all 16 full captured insertions at N=800/6400, plus six whole-graph
connection controls. Race-enabled capture and edge-case checks passed. Complete
preparation cost, durable insertion publication, retention and sustained-load
rescue remain open; finite equality is not a whole-goal completion.

[Private insertion link decisions](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_LINK_RESULTS.md)
now match 24 backend branch controls after separating selector target from
physical capacity. Construction comparison at the actual default level-zero
selector target also matches all 90 cases. Graph-wide insertion and durable
serving integration remain unfinished; no whole goal is closed.

[Backend construction comparison](research/public-task-pilot/CONSTRUCTION_BACKEND_COMPARISON.md)
found and fixed omitted backlink traversal. The corrected 90-case capture now
matches candidate and selected-neighbor sets/orders. Retrieval ID sets remain
unchanged, with modestly increased metric work. Initial invalid harness output
and the genuine pre-fix mismatch are retained and labeled. Full insertion and
sustained-load validation remain open.

[Private construction traversal](research/public-task-pilot/PRIVATE_CONSTRUCTION_TRAVERSAL_RESULTS.md)
now supports explicit start/level and literal construction ef. Focused race tests,
the ordinary research package suite, and captured retrieval comparison passed.
Backend construction equivalence, complete insertion and sustained-load rescue
remain open; none of the seven whole directions is completed by this component.
